==============================================================================

# Crawler identity, logs and safe edge access

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** CDN, reverse proxy and origin request telemetry  
**Evidence:** S08, S10, S11, S13, S17 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Distinguish claimed identity from verified identity

A request's user-agent text is a claim anyone can send. Store `claimed_agent` separately from `verification_result`, `verification_method` and `verification_data_date`. Use the provider's published verification mechanism; for Google, the official guidance includes IP-range and reverse/forward DNS approaches. [S13]

OpenAI, Anthropic and Perplexity document their crawler roles and access information. Recheck their official references rather than hardcoding an old list or assuming all traffic from a shared network belongs to one bot. [S08, S10, S11]

## Minimum useful log record

For authorized operations, record timestamp, host, path, status, response bytes, latency, cache outcome, claimed agent and verification result. Treat IP addresses and any query strings as potentially sensitive operational data: limit access and retention, redact secrets, and do not dump raw logs into marketing dashboards.

Group traffic by route class and verified consumer. Separate bot-policy rejection, WAF challenge, application error, rate limit and successful response. A 200 status alone does not prove useful content was returned; challenge pages and empty shells need content-aware checks.

## Edge policy design

Create narrow public-read exceptions only when justified. Do not bypass authentication, administrative restrictions or all security rules for a verified crawler. Keep protection of private resources independent from robots directives. Limit scope by host, method and route where the platform supports it.

Keep a last-known-good verification dataset and record refresh failures. Define what happens during provider outages or malformed updates. A failed refresh must not become `allow everyone`; a stale verification result must not silently become permanent truth.

## Operational tests

Use fixtures for a spoofed agent, an unrecognized IP, a verified crawler request, a protected route and a temporarily rate-limited route. Test both cached and origin responses. A command using a crawler user-agent string checks response behavior, not real crawler identity.

Compare successful retrieval rates and response usefulness before and after an edge change. Do not use crawl frequency as a proxy for citation frequency, human attention or revenue. A crawler may revisit a page without showing it to anyone.

## Handoff

Deliver the approved access policy, identity-verification method, log schema, dashboard definitions, evidence samples and rollback. Document missing visibility where a hosting platform does not expose the required fields. This package supplies the contract and fixtures, not a running client log collector or a verified account-level crawler report.

Title: Engine-specific discovery, training and user-access controls
Path: Library/Framework/framework-engine-control-matrix.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched

==============================================================================

# Engine-specific discovery, training and user-access controls

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Public website crawler policy; not authentication or access to private records  
**Evidence:** S03, S08, S10, S11, S12, S17 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Do not treat all AI traffic as one bot

| Consumer/token | Documented role | Policy decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Googlebot | Google Search crawling | Decide public Search crawl access; evaluate index/snippet rules separately. |
| Google-Extended | Gemini training and specified grounding uses; no separate HTTP user agent | Independent product-use choice; not a Google Search inclusion switch. |
| OAI-SearchBot | OpenAI search | Search discovery choice, separate from training. |
| GPTBot | OpenAI training | Training preference. |
| ChatGPT-User | User-triggered retrieval | Robots rules may not apply; access protection must not depend on robots. |
| Claude-SearchBot | Anthropic search | Search discovery choice. |
| ClaudeBot | Anthropic training | Training preference. |
| Claude-User | User-directed retrieval | Follow Anthropic's stated robots behavior; confirm real requests in logs. |
| PerplexityBot | Perplexity search | Search discovery, not foundation-model training. |
| Perplexity-User | User-directed retrieval | Generally ignores robots according to the provider. |

Roles above are from S03, S08, S10, S11 and S12. OAI-AdsBot is an ad-landing-page workflow, not a prerequisite for organic search visibility. Do not open it without a relevant advertising use case. [S08]

## Build a policy from business choices

Ask the owner separately about public discovery, training use, user-requested retrieval, paid-ad validation and private areas. Store the approved choices and the reason for each. A common example is allowing search discovery while opting out of training, but it is not a mandatory default and can restrict other grounding uses for some products.

Generate explicit groups from the approved policy. The supplied generator repeats protected-path crawl exclusions in specific groups rather than assuming every bot inherits the wildcard group. Never use robots to secure customer data, staging credentials, exports, unpublished drafts or administrative tools; enforce authentication and authorization at the application or edge.

Apply the file independently to the relevant hosts. Preview hosts, bare domains, www hosts and asset hosts can have different owners and access needs. Test the exact deployed origin and redirect behavior. Avoid accidentally publishing a staging-wide `Disallow: /` to the production hostname.

## Verification and safe rollout

Simulating a user agent checks a response variant, not the identity of a real crawler. Validate incoming identities using the provider's documented method and current published data. Cache verification metadata with a refresh timestamp and a last-known-good fallback. A failed refresh should not create a global allow rule.

Release a narrowly scoped policy change, monitor verified request success and record unexpected denials. Do not bypass authentication, malware defenses, rate limits or all WAF rules simply because a string resembles a crawler name. Crawler access, indexing, citation selection and customer conversion are different outcomes.

Acceptance evidence includes the approved policy JSON, generated robots file, host inventory, public/private route tests, edge rule diff and rollback instructions. This package provides examples, not an unapproved production policy.

Title: Evidence governance and claim authorization
Path: Library/Framework/framework-evidence-governance.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched

==============================================================================

# Evidence governance and claim authorization

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Every strategy, checklist, code example, sales statement and AI-assisted answer  
**Evidence:** S01, S02, S19, S29 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## One claim, one support record

The old library mixes engine requirements, useful house style, experiments and confident predictions. This edition uses separate evidence classes. **Official requirement** means a documented condition for a named feature. **Official recommendation** is guidance, not a promised result. **Primary research** is conditional on its experimental setup. **First-party observation** is a result from a specified client dataset. **Engineering judgment** is a proposed design decision. **Unverified legacy claim** is not authorized as client-facing fact.

Give every consequential rule a claim ID. Record the exact wording, intended consumer, affected page types, primary source, source date, retrieval date, confidence, implementation test, owner and conditions under which the claim would be withdrawn. A URL alone is insufficient: the cited passage must support the actual assertion. A requirement for a shopping result cannot be transferred to every service page.

## Review a numerical claim before using it

Extract the numerator, denominator, sample selection, date range, engine/model, geography, query mix, comparison method and uncertainty. Separate relative improvement from percentage-point improvement. Ask whether the observation measures citations, visible words, impressions, clicks or revenue. Missing denominators or inaccessible underlying evidence make a claim unverified; they do not establish that it is false.

The GEO paper is legitimate research, but its benchmark and deployed-engine experiments are not a universal 2026 commercial-search formula. Its results must remain tied to the tested setup and metrics. A patent describes an invention, not proof that a production system uses it. [S29]

## Conflict resolution

Prefer the most specific current official source for the exact product behavior. Preserve older sources when they explain history. Record why one source supersedes another rather than quietly changing dates. This release resolves the Google AI reporting conflict in favor of the June announcement and August rollout note, while retaining the earlier AI-features page for eligibility.

For competing independent studies, preserve both methods and do not average incompatible metrics. For company documentation versus logs, describe the documentation as stated behavior and logs as observed behavior; investigate identity, scope and timing before declaring a contradiction.

## Publication gate

A reviewer should be able to reproduce the reasoning without trusting the writer's authority. Replace unsupported absolutes such as “always,” “invisible,” “guaranteed,” and “exact algorithm” with a sourced scope or a testable hypothesis. Do not merely add “may” to a claim that has no supporting evidence.

An approved operational record contains a project-specific decision and acceptance test. Example: “SSR is selected for public service routes because the named retrieval clients need useful initial HTML; compare raw and rendered captures before release.” This is stronger engineering than pretending every crawler has identical rendering capabilities.

Use `Templates/claim-register.example.json` as the data contract. No automated score in this package certifies truth, ranking eligibility or legal compliance. Human review remains necessary for the claim itself.

==============================================================================

Title: Faceted navigation and crawl-space control
Path: Library/Framework/framework-faceted-url-contract.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Faceted navigation and crawl-space control

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Commerce filters, internal search, sorting and parameterized pages  
**Evidence:** S39, S15, S16 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Define which combinations deserve an independent page

Inventory filter dimensions, sorting modes, pagination, tracking parameters and variant selection. Estimate the generated URL space from actual application behavior, not only from links found in one crawl. Decide which combinations provide genuine search value and which exist only to help an on-site task.

Google's faceted-navigation guidance distinguishes cases where filtered URLs need search visibility from cases where unnecessary crawling should be prevented. Canonicals and indexing directives have separate roles and should not be used interchangeably. [S39, S15, S16]

## URL contract

For each parameter, record its meaning, allowed values, whether order matters, whether it changes content, intended index state and canonical rule. Reject impossible combinations gracefully. Prevent infinite calendars, repeating parameters and unbounded sort/filter permutations from producing apparently unique content indefinitely.

Do not erase parameters that control a legitimate user choice merely because they resemble tracking. Do not canonicalize every filtered page to a category if a selected subset is intentionally meant to be a distinct, useful search landing page. Conversely, do not index every possible permutation because it can be generated.

## Navigation and delivery

Keep important category and product discovery routes accessible through useful links. Ensure filter interactions work without trapping users or producing misleading back-button behavior. Preserve the selected state when appropriate and test empty-result states.

For desired indexable facets, provide stable URLs, useful visible content and clear internal placement. For utility-only states, implement the chosen control without hiding public value or blocking resources necessary to understand the page. A crawl exclusion does not authenticate the content or automatically remove an already indexed URL.

## Test fixtures

Include a normal category, one approved facet, a utility sort, a tracking parameter, invalid value, duplicate parameter, empty result and deep pagination. Verify status, canonical, links and intended index state. Test parameter ordering and encoding rather than assuming string-equivalent URLs always represent the same resource.

## Monitoring and rollback

Track growth in discovered parameter URLs, verified crawler load, error rates and useful landing-page performance. A rising URL count without added customer value can indicate a regression. Keep configuration changes reversible and preserve the old parameter contract for incident review.

Acceptance is a documented, bounded crawl space and correct behavior for intended page classes. It is not a promise that every allowed facet will be indexed or ranked. This module supplies the decision model; the actual rule set must be built from the client's application.

==============================================================================
DOCUMENT 054 OF 135
Title: Field performance and interaction acceptance
Path: Library/Framework/framework-field-performance-acceptance.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Field performance and interaction acceptance

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Core Web Vitals, frontend releases and experience budgets  
**Evidence:** S28 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Use the correct measurement layer

Current Core Web Vitals are LCP, INP and CLS. The good thresholds are LCP at most 2.5 seconds, INP at most 200 milliseconds and CLS at most 0.1, assessed at the 75th percentile with mobile and desktop segmentation. Lab checks are useful diagnostics but do not substitute for field evidence. [S28]

## Establish a baseline

Record route class, device group, observation period, sample volume, data source and metric definitions. Keep field observations separate from simulated tests. A fast office laptop running a single route does not represent every customer device or interaction.

Where field data is unavailable, explicitly label the gap. Use realistic lab scenarios to identify risks and collect permitted real-user data after release; do not invent a passing field score. A high Lighthouse result is not a certificate of search performance or accessibility.

## Prioritize meaningful interactions

Map the steps a customer must complete: navigate to a service, open a menu, compare a product, choose a variant, fill a form, submit and receive confirmation. Test these paths on constrained devices and realistic network conditions. A page can load quickly while an essential interaction stalls or fails.

For each issue, identify the responsible component, third-party script or server dependency. Distinguish rendering delay, slow server response, long tasks, unstable layout and network contention. Choose a change that addresses the measured cause rather than applying every optimization snippet indiscriminately.

## Budget and rollout

Set component or route budgets as project decisions. Include large hero media, fonts, consent tooling, chat widgets and analytics in the tested configuration, not only in a stripped-down demo. Compare the same measurement setup before and after the change.

Release to a controlled cohort where feasible and monitor both experience and business completion. A performance improvement that breaks form submission is not a successful release. Maintain a rollback for risky third-party or rendering changes.

## Reporting

Show percentile values, sample period and device cohort. Explain missing samples and major distribution changes. Track business outcomes separately; passing a threshold is not proof that an engine will rank the page higher. Use the observed user experience to justify the work rather than a promised ranking uplift.

The acceptance record includes the baseline, diagnostic evidence, code change, lab comparison, available field follow-up and unresolved limitations. This package contains a measurement procedure, not measured Core Web Vitals for any of Joseph's websites.

==============================================================================

# GA4 implementation — current AI channels, scopes and outcome quality

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched replacement of the legacy entry point  
**Primary sources:** S36 S37 S09 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Preservation:** The complete prior document remains byte-for-byte in `99-Originals/Framework/framework-ga4.md`. This replacement deliberately removes unsupported universal rules; it does not claim to have tested a client website.

## Start from business measurement

Define the customer action and the independently verifiable business result. A page view, button interaction, form submission, accepted lead and completed sale are different stages. Record which system owns each event and how duplicates, tests, spam and refunds are handled.

## Current channel and scope rules

The live GA4 documentation includes an AI Assistant default channel and keeps Google AI Overviews/AI Mode in Organic Search. First-user, session and event-scoped channel dimensions have different meanings. A custom AI channel is therefore an optional analytical choice, not a universal prerequisite for isolating all assistant traffic. [S36]

Use the current field definitions for the specific report or export. Do not recycle Universal Analytics session assumptions into GA4, and do not assert that a session restarts at midnight or every campaign change without the applicable GA4 source. The old document's session paragraph is superseded by this requirement to verify the actual contract.

## Collection and privacy

Specify the required events and the minimum permitted parameters. Keep personally identifying form values, email addresses and phone numbers out of analytics URLs, titles and event/campaign fields. Do not copy legacy analytics.js code from a general privacy article into a GA4 implementation. [S37]

Test both accepted and denied consent paths according to the project's approved implementation. Server-side collection does not remove the need to respect the applicable consent and privacy requirements. Do not disguise missing consented measurement as an observed visit or conversion.

## AI referral path

OpenAI documents a ChatGPT referral UTM convention. [S09] Test parameter preservation and collection through the real landing flow. Use current channel definitions and record changes over time. A missing referrer or later direct return cannot automatically be assigned to a particular AI platform.

## QA and reconciliation

Use clearly labeled test submissions and confirm that the business system receives the intended result. Compare browser collection, server processing and CRM outcomes without double counting. Confirm that a failed form does not emit the same success event as an accepted inquiry.

For ecommerce, reconcile accepted purchases and refunds against the transactional source. For lead generation, define qualification and the time required for review. Report the unresolved lead cohort rather than assuming all submissions qualify immediately.

## Analysis and handoff

Report acquisition and attribution with explicit scope and model settings. A platform-attributed result is not causal proof of incremental value. Keep measured visits, self-reported discovery and inferred exposure separately labeled.

Use [AI referral attribution](framework-ai-referral-attribution-2026.md), `Templates/metric-contracts.json` and the project intake template. This release does not install tags, inspect a GA4 account or claim measured performance for any client. It supplies the current analytical rules and acceptance workflow.

Title: Google generative AI performance: current reporting contract
Path: Library/Framework/framework-google-ai-performance-2026.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Google generative AI performance: current reporting contract

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Google Search Console, AI Overviews and AI Mode  
**Evidence:** S04, S05, S03 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Current feature boundary

Google announced dedicated generative AI performance reporting on June 3, 2026 and states that rollout reached websites worldwide on August 31. The Search report covers AI Overviews and AI Mode impressions, with page, country, device and date views. Insufficient impression volume can prevent a useful report from appearing. Search Labs experiments are excluded. [S04, S05]

Do not describe this as a Q4 2025 AI Overview tab, and do not assume dedicated clicks, CTR, raw prompts or separate AI Overview-versus-AI Mode dimensions. Use only fields exposed and documented for the property. The older guidance that AI activity contributes to overall Search reporting remains relevant, but is not evidence that dedicated impressions are unavailable. [S03, S04]

## Property setup and evidence capture

Confirm the site/property scope and permissions. Record the canonical host, domain versus URL-prefix property, timezone, available reports and earliest usable date. A rollout announcement is not a substitute for checking the client's property. Capture the report name and its visible dimensions before writing an export transformation.

Create a monthly snapshot with the period, comparison period, filters, data freshness, metric definition, export timestamp and excluded dates. Keep the unmodified export beside a normalized table. Retain the original URL value and a separately derived canonical URL key; do not destroy the source field during normalization.

## Analysis sequence

Start with the property's own distribution rather than a universal prevalence benchmark. Which relevant service, product or reference pages receive impressions? Which geography and device groups are represented? Where do changes coincide with a deployment, topic change or seasonality?

Compare a stable cohort of pages between periods. Separate newly published pages from the unchanged cohort so expansion does not masquerade as improvement to existing pages. Report absolute changes together with percentage changes; a rise from one impression to five is not strong business evidence even though the relative change is large.

Do not add AI impressions to overall impressions when they are already included in the overall report. Do not divide separately scoped analytics sessions by these impressions and label the result a platform CTR. If a field is absent, store `null` and explain that it is unavailable rather than inventing a value or treating it as zero.

## Decision rules

A sharp change first triggers checks for report scope, incomplete dates, URL consolidation, filters and site accessibility. It does not automatically prove an AI algorithm change. Content decisions require page relevance, evidence quality and business utility in addition to visibility.

The client deliverable should distinguish “observed in Google's AI features” from “clicked to the site” and “became a qualified lead.” Use `Templates/metric-contracts.json` and the AI attribution module for the latter layers. No Search Console account was connected or inspected as part of this library release.

==============================================================================

# Search Console analysis — current reports and defensible diagnostics

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched replacement of the legacy entry point  
**Primary sources:** S04 S05 S01 S43 S44 S15 S28 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Preservation:** The complete prior document remains byte-for-byte in `99-Originals/Framework/framework-gscanalysis.md`. This replacement deliberately removes unsupported universal rules; it does not claim to have tested a client website.

## Verify the property and the report

Start with the actual property scope, permissions, reporting dates and available features. A domain property, URL-prefix property and platform property do not automatically represent the same assets. Do not infer a client's data from a library example or an old screenshot.

The new generative AI report is documented in the June 2026 announcement and August 31 rollout note. The legacy document's Q4 2025 AI-tab timeline and broad list of retired search appearances are not carried forward. [S04, S05, S01]

## Diagnostic order

When performance changes, first validate the measurement: property, filters, incomplete dates, device/country mix, canonical aggregation and any report definition change. Then check technical availability, intended indexing state, content changes and business seasonality.

Do not use a single average position or aggregate CTR as an explanation for everything. Compare meaningful page and query cohorts where available. Keep new, unchanged and retired URLs separate to avoid confusing site expansion with improvement to existing pages.

## Indexing investigation

For an important URL, confirm the intended state, deployed status, public content, canonical relationship, internal discovery and available inspection evidence. Canonicalization is a signal, so compare the intended canonical with what the available report actually shows. [S15]

A low impression count does not authorize noindex or deletion. A technical problem requires a specific diagnosis, not a bulk action against every “thin” or low-traffic page. Record URL decisions through the lifecycle contract.

## Current additional surfaces

Use [Google AI performance](framework-google-ai-performance-2026.md) for dedicated AI impression reporting and its limits. Use [social/video properties](framework-social-video-properties.md) when the client's supported accounts are verified. Those platform properties measure discovery of those assets, not ordinary website behavior. [S43, S44]

Use field performance evidence correctly when interpreting Core Web Vitals. Do not equate a lab screenshot with a field report or infer that a threshold alone explains a ranking change. [S28]

## Reporting contract

Keep raw exports immutable. Store report name, metric definitions, filters, timezone, date range and extraction time with each analysis. Do not invent a universal query-anonymization cutoff or assume every UI field is available through an API/export channel without checking its own documentation.

A client summary should identify the strongest observed change, competing explanations, the action supported by evidence and what remains unknown. It should not label a suspected cause as a confirmed algorithm penalty.

No Search Console property was connected or audited during this library work. The deliverable is the updated analytical procedure and templates; real client measurements require the relevant access and source exports.

Title: Titles, descriptions and preview copy without false limits

Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Titles, descriptions and preview copy without false limits

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Public pages and template-generated search metadata  
**Evidence:** S34, S35 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Write for the page and reader

Google does not impose a fixed 50–60-character title requirement or a 140–160-character description requirement. Display may be truncated and the search system may choose other text. Use character counts as an editorial review aid, not a technical pass/fail rule. [S34, S35]

Start from the page's actual purpose and the customer's question. Write a distinctive title that identifies the service, product or information. Use a concise description that explains the useful content and any relevant limitation. Do not promise a price, location, qualification or outcome that the page cannot substantiate.

## Template contract

Define how static brand text combines with the page-specific title. Test missing values, long names, multilingual text and characters requiring HTML escaping. Avoid output such as an empty separator, duplicated brand name or a description copied from an unrelated page.

Store the source fields separately from rendered tags. A frontend component, CMS plugin and third-party head manager should not all emit conflicting titles or descriptions. Ensure the completed document contains the intended output for each relevant consumer and rendering path.

## Review process

Compare a sample of neighboring pages side by side. Can a person distinguish them without opening the page? Do they describe genuinely different content? A repeated business name is reasonable; repetitive boilerplate that hides the page's identity is not useful.

Preview likely display widths, but do not shorten necessary meaning simply to satisfy one simulated pixel limit. Search presentation varies by query and device. Keep visible headings and metadata consistent without mechanically forcing every field to contain the same exact phrase.

## Testing and acceptance

Check for an empty title, duplicate title elements, missing descriptions where required by the project, invalid escaping and unresolved placeholders. Treat length outliers as review notes. A correctly implemented description may still not be used by a search engine; acceptance should concern truthful, reliable metadata output rather than a guaranteed snippet.

The supplied HTML smoke tool checks limited saved-document conditions. It does not predict a search snippet or measure click-through rate. Follow actual page performance after release, considering query mix and ranking changes before attributing a CTR movement to copy alone.

Title: Location-page evidence and genuine local utility

Label: NEW · researched

==============================================================================

# Location-page evidence and genuine local utility

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Service-area businesses, real offices and regional landing pages  
**Evidence:** S18, S32, S33, S20 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## A location is not a keyword permutation

Approve a location page only when it answers a real local customer need and the business can substantiate its relevance. Useful evidence may include actual service coverage, local projects with permission, travel or scheduling constraints, region-specific service details, legitimate contacts and accurate customer-facing information.

Google's doorway policy addresses substantially similar location-targeted pages that funnel users without adequate utility. Business Profile guidance requires accurate real-world representation. A service area does not automatically establish a staffed storefront. [S18, S32, S33]

## Page-creation dossier

Before writing, record the location, offered service, customer question, operational coverage evidence, responsible reviewer and the unique information the page will provide. Identify what is genuinely different from the parent service page. Where there is no meaningful difference, prefer a useful coverage section rather than another indexable page.

Do not invent office addresses, staff, local testimonials, licenses, project photos or business history. Obtain approval to publish customer examples and remove identifying details when necessary. Structured data must match the real business and visible page; it is not a license to manufacture a location. [S20]

A schema graph can describe places and their relationships without requiring a separate indexed page for every state, county and city. Use stable identifiers for genuine entities. Create a hierarchy because it helps people browse, not because a presumed algorithm needs every geographic level.

## Content and layout

Lead with the service and location-specific facts a customer needs to make a decision. Provide clear scope, contact options, constraints and relevant supporting work. Use maps and photographs only when they represent reality. A generic stock image does not become local evidence by changing its alt text.

Avoid mechanically swapping place names through otherwise identical prose. Repeated service facts are not inherently wrong, but the page as a whole must justify its independent existence. Editorial review should compare neighboring pages side by side and ask whether a customer gains useful information from each.

## Acceptance and monitoring

The acceptance package contains the evidence dossier, approved business facts, a comparison with neighboring pages, schema validation scope and the intended index state. Monitor qualified inquiries and incorrect-location contacts as well as visibility; attracting customers in an unserved area can be a failure.

For existing pages, evaluate individually. Consolidation may be sensible where independent utility is absent, but do not delete every low-traffic city page automatically. Preserve genuine customer needs and incoming references through the URL-lifecycle process. No new local addresses or client-specific coverage claims have been invented in this edition.

Title: Migration contract, checkpoints and rollback
Path: Library/Framework/framework-migration-rollback-contract.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Migration contract, checkpoints and rollback

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Domain, URL, CMS, rendering and hosting migrations  
**Evidence:** S38, S15, S16 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Treat a migration as a controlled state transition

Write down the old and new states: domains, URL patterns, content, rendering, infrastructure, analytics, structured data and business workflows. Avoid changing all of them at once without a clear reason and an evidence plan. Google's migration guidance emphasizes preparation, mapping and monitoring; no checklist guarantees unchanged rankings. [S38]

## Required inputs

Export the known URL inventory from multiple available sources, such as the CMS, sitemap, crawl, analytics and link records. Label each source's completeness. Build an explicit old-to-new map with actions for retained, consolidated, moved and removed content.

Inventory verification files, robots rules, canonical generation, hreflang, feeds, media URLs, forms, redirects and transactional emails. Include the bare/www variants and subdomains actually involved. Do not infer that a single main-host test covers the entire estate.

## Preflight

Test representative routes and high-value edge cases in the new environment. Confirm public content, status behavior, internal links, metadata and business transactions. Protect staging through appropriate access controls; do not rely solely on robots, and ensure any intended production indexing rules are prepared separately.

Canonical and indexing decisions need explicit intent. A migration must not accidentally retain a staging canonical, leak an environment hostname or ship `noindex` to public routes. [S15, S16]

## Cutover and rollback

Record who can approve cutover, what evidence is required and which failures trigger rollback. Examples of blocking operational failures include widespread errors, broken checkout, missing public content or an unintended host redirect. Define the rollback steps for code, DNS/edge configuration, content and caches; they may have different propagation characteristics.

Keep the old mapping and configuration accessible. A rollback is not “restore everything” unless the team knows exactly which state to restore. Rehearse a small representative recovery in a safe environment where practical.

## Post-launch verification

Repeat the preflight tests against the actual production hostname. Check form delivery, transactions, redirect destinations, canonical output and verification access. Then monitor crawl/indexing signals and business outcomes over appropriate periods. Distinguish incomplete reporting from an actual loss.

Maintain a migration ledger with deployment time, observed issues, fixes and source exports. Do not claim a traffic change is caused by the migration until measurement, seasonality and concurrent changes are considered. Acceptance means the agreed technical and business behavior is demonstrated; it does not mean the search engines have promised a specific result.

==============================================================================

Title: Next.js metadata, streaming and rendering validation
Path: Library/Framework/framework-nextjs-metadata-validation.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Next.js metadata, streaming and rendering validation

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Next.js App Router and React public-content routes  
**Evidence:** S14, S24, S25, S26, S27 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Pin the implementation context

Record the installed Next.js and React versions, router, runtime, hosting target, cache strategy and deployment adapter. Do not copy a server configuration from another client's machine or assume that a framework version mentioned in an old document matches the project.

Next.js supports metadata streaming and has an `htmlLimitedBots` configuration introduced in 15.2. A custom expression replaces its default list rather than extending it automatically. The official documentation recommends leaving the default sufficient for most cases. [S25–S27]

## Route contract

For each public route, identify the content owner, metadata source, intended canonical, social preview and required initial content. Static metadata is appropriate when the values are static; dynamic metadata should be derived from the actual route record. Ensure invalid identifiers lead to the intended missing-page behavior rather than fabricated titles or successful empty pages.

Keep canonical and visible product/article identity consistent. Do not generate one canonical in the initial response and change it to a conflicting value after hydration. Google can render JavaScript, but that does not prove every retrieval client sees the same content or behaves identically. [S14]

## Capture the right evidence

Test a normal browser and the specific limited consumers relevant to the project. Save complete response bodies, response timing, headers and rendered output; a first-chunk-only grep can misread streamed metadata. Distinguish “not in the first chunk” from “not delivered at all.” A spoofed user agent is a response-variant test, not proof that a real provider fetched the page.

Check title, description, canonical, language, robots directives, Open Graph fields and JSON-LD for duplicates and contradictions. Use a slow content source and a failure fixture to test error behavior. Recheck after a content update to expose cache-invalidation problems.

## Configuration changes

Do not disable metadata streaming globally simply because a legacy checklist says all tags must be in the first byte. Establish a failing consumer case, compare alternatives and measure the latency tradeoff. If overriding `htmlLimitedBots`, preserve the intended consumers and verify the resulting behavior against the pinned release.

## Release result

The acceptance package contains version information, template tests, raw and rendered captures, exceptions and the reason for any advanced override. The offline HTML checker in this release can inspect a saved complete response but cannot execute the Next.js app or certify streaming behavior. Browser and production-edge tests remain required.

React itself is not an SEO verdict. Choose CSR, SSG, SSR or a hybrid based on route purpose, user experience and the consumers that must retrieve it. Treat rendering choice as an engineering decision with evidence, not a claim that every public CSR route is inherently professional malpractice. [S24]

==============================================================================
DOCUMENT 063 OF 135
Title: Next.js SEO implementation — versioned route contracts
Path: Library/Framework/framework-nextjs.md
Group: baseline
Label: REWRITTEN · researched
Review status: rewritten_current_guidance
==============================================================================

# Next.js SEO implementation — versioned route contracts

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched replacement of the legacy entry point  
**Primary sources:** S14 S24 S25 S26 S27 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Preservation:** The complete prior document remains byte-for-byte in `99-Originals/Framework/framework-nextjs.md`. This replacement deliberately removes unsupported universal rules; it does not claim to have tested a client website.

## Record the environment before writing code

Capture the installed Next.js/React versions, App versus Pages Router, runtime, deployment adapter and caching strategy. An older nginx/PM2 example is not a requirement for every Next.js deployment. Choose the project's actual supported hosting path.

Use the framework's current APIs for the pinned release. A snippet that compiles against another major version is not ready merely because its comments say “2026.” Check route parameters, data fetching, metadata behavior and error handling against the installed version.

## Public route contract

For each route class define the source record, meaningful content, canonical, indexing intent, social metadata, structured data and user action. Use static generation or server rendering where it provides the required delivery reliability; use client interaction where it serves the task. React itself supports multiple rendering approaches through frameworks. [S24]

Missing records need the intended not-found behavior, not an empty successful template. Redirects must follow the approved URL map. Do not emit a staging domain, unresolved slug or default product name as production metadata.

## Metadata and streaming

Next.js documents metadata streaming and limited-bot handling. The `htmlLimitedBots` option, introduced in 15.2, replaces the default list when customized. Do not blindly add a partial regex and assume the framework keeps all existing matches. [S25–S27]

Use [Next.js metadata validation](framework-nextjs-metadata-validation.md) for complete-response captures and consumer-specific tests. A first-byte grep is not a sufficient streaming test. Disabling streaming globally is an advanced tradeoff requiring evidence, not a mandatory SEO improvement.

## Structured data and content

Generate public JSON-LD from the same approved record as the visible page. Serialize safely, including escaping `<` when embedding untrusted text in a script element. Avoid duplicating a graph across CMS and application layers with conflicting identities.

Google can render JavaScript; this does not prove every consumer will execute the same code or see the same output. Test the actual route against its required consumers and preserve useful content when nonessential scripts fail. [S14]

## Caching and release

Identify which content can be cached, what invalidates it and how changes reach page HTML, metadata and feeds. Test stale data, missing data and failed upstream requests. Do not apply `no-store` to every page by default or cache personalized content publicly because a generic guide says caching helps SEO.

Run route, accessibility, business-function and live-edge checks before accepting the release. The package's offline tools inspect saved artifacts only; they do not execute a Next.js build. Client-specific implementation remains a separate step grounded in the actual codebase and installed dependencies.

Title: Preferred Sources and publisher relationship design

Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched
Review status: new_researched_module
==============================================================================

# Preferred Sources and publisher relationship design

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Eligible publisher sites and reader-facing preference controls  
**Evidence:** S45, S43, S44 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## A reader preference is not an SEO switch

Google provides a Preferred Sources workflow and publisher guidance, including an interactive button option. The feature is based on reader choices and supported Search contexts. Do not market a button installation as guaranteed placement, universal availability or a substitute for useful publishing. [S45]

## Applicability assessment

Verify the current feature's domain, page and market requirements from the official guide before implementation. Confirm that the client is a relevant publisher and that the destination identifies the correct publication. A generic service business may gain more from a better service page than from a publisher-specific feature.

Record the intended reader action and why it is useful. Use clear wording that tells the reader what the preference affects. Do not obscure the destination, imitate a browser warning or make the action appear necessary to access ordinary content.

## Design and implementation

Follow the current official integration method rather than embedding a stale copied URL pattern. Keep the control accessible and visually distinct from primary navigation or a purchase action. Test keyboard access, mobile layout, loading failure and the destination on the supported surfaces.

Avoid introducing heavy scripts or layout shifts for a secondary preference action. Preserve core content when the integration fails. Do not require an unrelated subscription or collect unnecessary personal information merely to expose the preference control.

## Measurement

Track an approved button interaction separately from a confirmed preference or later Search exposure. A click on the button does not prove the user completed the preference flow, and subsequent traffic cannot automatically be attributed to it. Use the current platform report definitions and disclose any unavailable outcome.

Where the publication also has supported social/video profiles, maintain consistent factual identity and evaluate their Search Console properties separately. Those reports measure different assets and should not be combined into a fabricated publisher-authority score. [S43, S44]

## Acceptance

Deliver the applicability record, official-source date, approved UI copy, tested destination, accessibility evidence and measurement limitations. Remove or revise the integration if its official contract changes. This library release adds the workflow but does not install a button or change any publisher account.

# Regional Search eligibility and policy differences

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** International clients and geographically limited search features  
**Evidence:** S41, S42, S40 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Keep three geographies separate

Record the business's operating jurisdiction, the content's intended market and the searcher's location. They are not interchangeable. A feature may depend on user geography, language, query category and provider participation even when the website is technically correct.

Google's regional-differences documentation describes feature-specific availability rather than one worldwide Search experience. The August 2026 site-reputation policy update also changes treatment in relation to the EEA. Do not infer that a rule applies everywhere from one local screenshot, or that regional enforcement differences authorize abusive content. [S41, S42]

## Eligibility register

Create one row per proposed feature: engine, feature, content type, target countries, languages, required partner/account participation, required data, evidence source, source date and observed availability. Use `unknown` where a condition has not been verified. Do not market an unavailable feature as part of a guaranteed deliverable.

For international URLs, define the canonical and alternate-language relationships before implementation. Hreflang is for localized alternatives; it is not a substitute for translation quality or a technique for making unrelated pages equivalent. Test reciprocity and URL validity using the selected implementation method. [S40]

## Research and deployment workflow

Begin with the current official feature documentation, then verify the client's actual account and market. Keep compliance review separate from technical eligibility. For example, a technically valid product offer may still need business approval for local terms, currency, tax presentation or availability.

Test representative locale routes without forcing the tester through an irreversible geolocation redirect. Ensure a customer can select the appropriate language/market. Record how the application behaves when location cannot be inferred, a locale is unsupported or a product is unavailable.

## Policy incidents

When a manual action or policy notice appears, preserve its exact text, affected scope, date and property. Analyze the specific issue instead of applying an old universal recovery recipe. An EEA-related enforcement distinction concerns the documented treatment; it is not a promise that an affected section will retain visibility.

Coordinate SEO, operations and qualified legal/compliance review for jurisdiction-specific decisions. This module is a technical research workflow, not legal advice. Do not remove user protections or fabricate editorial independence to chase a regional exception.

Acceptance requires a signed eligibility register, locale test evidence, source dates and a clear distinction between documented availability and observed client access. A globally deployed website is not evidence that every Search feature is globally available.

# Research maintenance and documentation drift control

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Ongoing stewardship of this library  
**Evidence:** S01, S02, S04, S05, S47 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Use a source ledger rather than a refresh slogan

Each maintained rule needs an owner, current source, observed date, affected modules and a next-review condition. A document is not current merely because its title includes the year. Preserve the difference between publication date, source update date, retrieval date and the effective date of a product change.

This release encountered a concrete example: older Google AI documentation described overall reporting, while a later announcement and help page document a dedicated report. Keep a conflict record so the next editor does not accidentally restore the older interpretation. [S03–S05 in the source register]

## Proposed review cadence

Review engine bot controls, eligibility changes and critical policies before each relevant deployment and on a recurring team-owned schedule. Review framework-specific examples when the installed major version changes. Review business facts when their source changes. These are recommended operating practices; no background monitoring service has been scheduled by this package.

Use the official changelog as a trigger, not a substitute for reading the affected page. A documentation clarification may not mean a new ranking factor or a newly launched feature. Record the event type explicitly. [S01]

## Change workflow

Open a change record with the source, exact changed claim, affected modules, urgency and test impact. Update the canonical owner first, then dependent tiers, templates, sales wording and acceptance tests. Keep a concise before/after explanation and the original evidence location.

For protocol and software changes, distinguish stable, older supported and draft versions. A draft security page can inform a threat review, but should not silently redefine a pinned production implementation. The MCP module intentionally records version boundaries. [S47]

## Automated checks

Run the supplied library auditor for local file links, JSON syntax, metadata references and preserved-original hashes. These checks do not confirm that an external page is still live or that its content supports a claim. External link checking and semantic source review are separate activities.

Maintain a queue for unverified historical claims. A regex match is a lead for review, not a confirmed defect. Close a claim only after a reviewer records a disposition: verified, narrowed, superseded, removed or still unresolved.

## Acceptance

Every release should carry a manifest, migration map, source register, coverage matrix and test report. Mark retained legacy documents honestly instead of assigning a current research stamp to untouched prose. Preserve rollback copies and make the authoritative entry point obvious to both humans and AI assistants.

The objective is controlled, traceable improvement, not endless document growth. Add a new module only when it owns a distinct decision, implementation contract or evidence workflow.

Title: Structured-data lifecycle and feature eligibility
Path: Library/Framework/framework-schema-lifecycle.md
Group: baseline
Label: NEW · researched

==============================================================================

# Structured-data lifecycle and feature eligibility

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Every schema template and client deliverable  
**Evidence:** S01, S20, S21, S22, S23 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Three separate questions

Ask whether the JSON is syntactically valid, whether the vocabulary describes the page truthfully, and whether a named consumer currently supports a related feature. These are separate checks. Passing a generic validator does not establish rich-result eligibility or guarantee appearance. [S20]

Google stopped showing FAQ rich results on May 7, 2026. HowTo rich results were retired in September 2023. This does not make the underlying Schema.org vocabulary invalid, but these Google features must not be sold as current benefits. [S01, S21]

## Registry design

For every template, maintain type, consumer, feature, required properties, recommended properties, page-class applicability, policy constraints, current status, source date, test method and owner. Mark entries as supported, restricted, deprecated, vocabulary-only or unverified. Avoid a single “schema installed” column.

Keep eligibility logic next to the generator. A Product template needs genuine product information and a current offer when it represents a purchasable item; a LocalBusiness template must not manufacture a storefront; an Article must identify its actual content and authorship. Optional ratings should never be filled with invented values simply to satisfy an example.

## Change management

When a feature is retired, search the library, sales scope, QA tests and codebase for the feature name. Update expected outcomes and acceptance criteria. Do not automatically delete useful customer-facing questions or step-by-step instructions because a search decoration disappeared.

If markup still serves another documented consumer, retain it with that consumer and rationale recorded. Otherwise evaluate maintenance cost against actual use. Remove dead code through a tested change, not a blind text replacement across every type name.

## Review integrity

Review and rating markup must reflect genuine visible evidence. Google's review guidance excludes fake or undisclosed incentivized reviews and restricts self-serving LocalBusiness/Organization star eligibility. [S22] Disclosing an incentive is not permission to fabricate an experience or ignore another platform's stricter policy.

## Acceptance evidence

Save representative rendered pages, generated JSON-LD, validator results, policy checklist and source version. For commerce, compare offer values with the live landing page and approved feed. Record known warnings rather than creating fake data to eliminate them. For large deployments, test edge cases such as missing images, unavailable products, absent ratings, multiple authors and multilingual URLs.

The `Schema.org` directory contains small fictional examples and a release checklist. They are intentionally not marked as Google-approved or production-ready. Replace example facts, validate the intended feature and verify the visible page before publishing. The library's schema work is semantic modeling and eligibility engineering, not a promised citation multiplier.

# Schema types in 2026 — semantic validity versus search features

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched replacement of the legacy entry point  
**Primary sources:** S01 S20 S21 S22 S23 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Preservation:** The complete prior document remains byte-for-byte in `99-Originals/Framework/framework-schema-types-2026.md`. This replacement deliberately removes unsupported universal rules; it does not claim to have tested a client website.

## A type catalog is not a benefit catalog

Schema.org vocabulary and search-engine feature support are separate layers. A valid type can describe content even when Google does not offer a corresponding rich result. Feature eligibility additionally depends on required properties, policy, content and availability. Appearance is not guaranteed. [S20]

## Current planning matrix

| Family | Use in this library | Acceptance boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Organization / WebSite | Model genuine identity and site relationships | Real identifiers and visible business facts; no knowledge-panel promise. |
| Article / Person | Model actual editorial content and authorship | Real author/content relationship; no fabricated credentials. |
| BreadcrumbList | Describe the real navigational hierarchy | Match meaningful navigation and valid URLs. |
| Product / Offer | Describe a genuine product and commercial offer | Follow the current feature guide and reconcile page, feed and checkout. |
| LocalBusiness | Describe a legitimate applicable business entity | No invented storefronts, addresses or local staff. |
| Review / AggregateRating | Describe authentic qualifying reviews | Apply current feature policy; no placeholder ratings. |
| FAQPage | Optional vocabulary where justified | Google FAQ rich results retired May 7, 2026. |
| HowTo | Optional vocabulary for genuine instructions | Google HowTo rich results retired September 2023. |
| Other types | Evaluate individually | Consult the current specific consumer guide before promising a feature. |

FAQ and HowTo lifecycle facts are from S01 and S21. Do not copy an old rich-result checklist into a 2026 proposal. Do not assume that removal of one type implies every neighboring feature, such as all book-related integrations, is also retired.

## Model only supported facts

Use the most accurate applicable type without inventing a subtype to appear sophisticated. Populate properties from controlled records. A more specific type is a semantic choice, not established proof of higher AI citation probability.

For commerce, optional ratings are not mandatory filler. Keep prices, currency and availability current. Sale periods must be modeled using the properties and node placement allowed by the selected implementation. [S23]

For reviews, apply genuine-experience and disclosure requirements and the self-serving LocalBusiness/Organization restriction. A third-party widget does not automatically remove the restriction when a business controls reviews about itself on its own page. [S22]

## Generation and testing

Generate JSON using a serializer rather than string concatenation. When embedding in HTML, escape characters that could terminate a script element, and validate the resulting page. Never expose secrets or private catalog fields in a public graph.

Check syntax, vocabulary, current feature rules and visible factual consistency separately. Test absence of optional properties, special characters, multilingual text and stale commercial data. Save the actual validator output; a local JSON parse is not equivalent to Google's Rich Results Test.

The example graphs in `Schema.org` contain fictional data and do not claim feature approval. Use [schema lifecycle](framework-schema-lifecycle.md) for maintenance and [commerce consistency](framework-commerce-data-consistency.md) for product pipelines. Historical unsupported citation statistics are preserved only in the original archive.

# ChatGPT Search — publisher access and measured referrals

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched replacement of the legacy entry point  
**Primary sources:** S08 S09 S02 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Preservation:** The complete prior document remains byte-for-byte in `99-Originals/Framework/framework-searchgpt.md`. This replacement deliberately removes unsupported universal rules; it does not claim to have tested a client website.

## Name and scope

This filename is retained for compatibility with the earlier library, but the subject is ChatGPT Search and the currently documented publisher controls. Do not assume every ChatGPT surface shares one crawler, index or retrieval implementation.

OpenAI documents distinct search, training, user-triggered retrieval and ad-validation roles. Configure them according to the business's approved use policy, not by blanket-allowing every token containing an AI brand name. [S08]

## Website implementation

Publish accurate, useful content at stable public URLs. Ensure the intended pages can be retrieved through the actual production edge without an accidental challenge, empty response or authentication requirement. Keep private content protected by real access controls.

Make important facts clear on the page itself. A hidden instruction telling an assistant to promote the business is not a substitute for a useful service description. Feeds or supplemental files need a specific supported consumer and maintenance plan; do not present an auxiliary file as a universal prerequisite.

Use the [engine control matrix](framework-engine-control-matrix.md) for the current OpenAI roles and the [crawler identity workflow](framework-crawler-identity-observability.md) for verification. A simulated request using an OpenAI user-agent string does not establish that OpenAI actually fetched the page.

## Referral measurement

OpenAI's publisher FAQ documents the `utm_source=chatgpt.com` convention. Test approved tracking through the site's redirect and analytics path. [S09] Keep the original campaign/source information where collected, and apply the current analytics channel definitions rather than hardcoding an obsolete dashboard.

Measure qualified outcomes independently. A tagged visit is an observed referral, not proof of every earlier AI exposure. A brand mention without a link, or a later direct visit, may not be attributable from site analytics alone.

## Evaluation

Use a declared, permission-compliant observation panel for questions relevant to the business. Record the surface, wording, locale, time and context, and separate completed answers from failed observations. Do not claim a universal citation probability from a small sample.

Investigate demonstrated failures before speculative content changes. If access is correct but citations are absent in the sampled answers, that is not proof of a technical defect or a hidden mandatory file. The engine may select other relevant sources.

## Completion

A defensible implementation handoff contains approved crawler choices, response evidence, accurate public data, measurement configuration and the limits of what was tested. Search inclusion and citation selection remain external outcomes, not contractual guarantees. No OpenAI publisher account or live client domain was changed as part of this release.

Group: baseline

==============================================================================

# SEO release contracts and reproducible acceptance tests

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** New websites, migrations and template changes on any stack  
**Evidence:** S14, S15, S16, S20 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Replace vague completion with evidence

“SEO installed” is not a release criterion. Define contracts for each page class: public service, product, article, location, search/filter, account, error and retired URL. Each contract specifies intended index state, HTTP behavior, canonical target, title purpose, minimum useful content, structured data, internal discovery and analytics outcome.

The underlying controls have different functions: rendering affects what a consumer receives, canonicalization expresses consolidation preferences, and indexing/snippet directives control eligible use. Structured data has its own feature-specific rules. [S14–S16, S20]

## Test matrix

For every template, include at least one normal route, one boundary case and one failure case. Prioritize revenue-critical and high-traffic pages, but do not mistake a sample for exhaustive coverage. Record sample selection in the evidence package.

Capture status and headers from the actual response. Save initial HTML and browser-rendered HTML separately. Compare meaningful text, links, canonical and metadata. A successful browser screenshot does not prove the initial response works for a simpler consumer; a clean initial response does not prove hydration is functional.

Run the supplied offline HTML smoke checker against saved files. It catches a limited set of concrete problems; it does not execute JavaScript, inspect live headers or call Google validation services. A passing result is evidence only for the checks performed. Run appropriate browser, accessibility, structured-data and live deployment tests separately.

## Blocking defects versus advisory findings

For an intended public page, accidental `noindex`, an unexpected redirect, an empty title or a broken canonical may block the project release under the agreed contract. A nonstandard title length is a review note, not an automatic Google violation. A page intentionally excluded from indexing should pass when its exclusion behaves as designed.

Require sign-off for destructive changes: bulk redirects, noindex rules, URL-case normalization, sitemap removal and deletion. Keep an old/new mapping and rollback plan. Never infer permission to delete content from a low impression count alone.

## Release evidence

Store the commit identifier, build/runtime version, environment, test time, URL sample, raw captures, automated output, manual reviewer and unresolved exceptions. An exception needs an owner, reason and expiration date. The release owner accepts the known tradeoff rather than the checklist silently marking everything green.

After deployment, repeat the same critical-route tests against production. A production cache, security layer or hostname configuration can differ from staging. Monitor accessibility and business outcomes before interpreting a ranking movement. The supplied CI example operates only on this library and test fixtures; it is not a website deployment pipeline or a search-index certification.

# Social and video platform properties in Search Console

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Supported publisher/business profiles and video discovery  
**Evidence:** S43, S44, S50 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Separate Google discovery from platform-native performance

Google's July 29, 2026 announcement describes globally available platform properties for supported social/video services, including Instagram, TikTok, X and YouTube. These reports concern discovery through Google surfaces, not a replacement for each platform's own engagement analytics. [S43, S44]

## Ownership and setup

Create an inventory of legitimate client profiles, account owners, access permissions and official website relationships. Verify the relevant account using the current product workflow. Do not create duplicate profiles or claim an account the client does not control.

Record the exact profile identifier and property scope. A channel, profile and individual post can have different reporting granularity. Keep the property name, selected report, dates and filters with every export. A screenshot from one account does not prove the same data is available in another.

## Content mapping

Map each profile and important content item to its business purpose and related website page where appropriate. Use clear, consistent factual identity and a useful destination. Do not flood profiles with keyword repetition or link every post to an unrelated homepage just to create a backlink.

For video hosted on the client's site, evaluate watch-page eligibility, stable thumbnails and accessible media according to Google's video guidance. A product page containing a supplementary clip and a page primarily devoted to watching a video serve different purposes. [S50]

## Reporting workflow

Track discovery impressions/clicks where the selected report exposes them, then separately examine native-platform views, engagement and website referrals. Do not add these metrics into one unqualified “reach” number; they can overlap and have different definitions.

Compare content cohorts by topic and user intent. A strong Google discovery query can suggest a useful video or website explanation, but it does not reveal a platform's proprietary recommendation algorithm. Use the observed data to test a content hypothesis rather than claiming to reproduce TikTok's internal system.

## Acceptance and maintenance

The deliverable includes verified-profile scope, evidence of available reporting, content-to-business mapping, metric definitions and a list of actions supported by the data. Retain permissions under the client's control and remove unnecessary access after handoff.

This module adds a current reporting opportunity to the library. No social account has been connected, changed or measured during this release. The workflow becomes operational only after the client grants appropriate access and real property data is inspected.

# First-party facts, provenance and reusable evidence

**Edition:** 2026.09.09 · **Status:** researched guidance and proposed operating procedure  
**Applies to:** Service pages, case studies, comparison pages and factual datasets  
**Evidence:** S19, S20, S29 in the [source register](../../Research/SOURCE-REGISTER.md).  
**Boundary:** Official facts are attributed below. Acceptance gates, priorities and workflows are agency recommendations, not secret ranking factors or performance guarantees.

## Build a source of truth, not a citation costume

A website should draw business facts from approved records: services actually offered, real qualifications, current availability, genuine project outcomes and verifiable contact details. Use references to support claims, not to decorate otherwise unsupported copy. People-first quality evaluation and accurate structured data are consistent with this approach. [S19, S20]

## Fact record

Store a fact ID, exact statement, subject/entity, evidence owner, source location, source date, approved public wording, privacy restrictions, expiration condition and affected pages. Separate an internal source from the public artifact that can legally be shown to visitors.

For a case study, record what happened, the baseline, the time period, the measurement system, the denominator, other simultaneous changes and client permission. Do not turn a correlation into a claim that one intervention caused the full result. A business testimonial is not an independently verified measurement.

## Reuse without contradiction

Connect the approved fact record to page copy, metadata, structured data, downloadable material and relevant feeds. Update dependent representations together. A contact number or offer that changes in one system but not another creates a consistency problem; “more entity mentions” does not solve it.

Preserve edit history. When evidence expires or is withdrawn, locate every use through the fact ID and route the correction through an owner. Keep a correction note when the public claim materially changes. Do not merely refresh the date while leaving stale information intact.

## Content design

Place the relevant answer and supporting context where a reader can understand them. Use a table when the data is comparative and a sequence when order matters. Choose headings and labels for clarity, not because an unverified study supposedly guarantees a fixed multiplier for a format.

A useful reference page can include definitions, limitations, examples and source context. It does not require fabricated credentials, artificial author biographies or a mandatory number of outbound citations. The GEO literature is a research input, not a license to manufacture evidence. [S29]

## Acceptance

A reviewer should trace each important public claim back to an approved record. Check rights to images, testimonials and excerpts. Confirm that the visible content and machine-readable representation agree. Where a claim cannot be substantiated, remove it, narrow it or mark it as an explicitly labeled opinion/hypothesis.

The handoff is a fact register, publishing map, review ownership and correction workflow. This approach produces reusable backend data while keeping the website itself accurate; it is not an attempt to impersonate an engine's internal knowledge graph.

Title: Search spam policy — scoped interpretation and operational controls
